MSCI 降評風險升高，印尼股市保守看待
2026 年 10 月 27 日
一、 市場動態
1. MSCI 擬將印尼降至獨立市場，降評風險明顯升高：MSCI 於 10 月 15 日宣布，啟動
將印尼股市由「新興市場（Emerging Market）」 直接降至「獨立市場（Standalone
Market）」的諮詢程序，意見徵詢將於 2027 年 4 月 30 日截止，最晚於 2027 年 6 月
30 日公布最終決定。MSCI 原先於今年 1 月提出可能降至「邊境市場（Frontier Market
）」，此次則跳過「邊境市場」提出直接將該國股市降至「獨立市場」的方案，顯示對

印尼市場治理的疑慮進一步加深。雖然「邊境市場」仍可能是替代分類方案，但 MSCI
強調，除非有明確證據顯示市場治理及運作出現實質改善，否則預期將依目前提案重
新分類，顯示此次降評風險較過去顯著升高。
2. 股權透明度與交易秩序問題為 MSCI 主要疑慮：MSCI 指出，印尼股市長期存在股權
結構不透明、實際自由流通股比例難以確認、疑似協調交易，以及市場監理執法成效
不足等問題，影響國際投資人對市場公平性與可投資性的信心。雖然印尼主管機關已
提出強化持股超過 1%股東資訊揭露、建立高持股集中度（HSC）監理機制，以及規
劃逐步將最低自由流通股比例提高至 15%等改革措施，但 MSCI 認為相關改革尚未展
現持續且具實質性的改善成果。由於問題涉及市場制度與治理結構，改善所需時間可
能較一般短期市場波動更長。
3. 股市反彈後再度回落，外資撤離壓力仍高：受降評消息衝擊，印尼雅加達綜合指數 10
月 16 日重挫 11.57%，創 1998 年以來最大單日跌幅，19 日再跌 3.64%，盤中最低

觸及 4,907.37 點。雖然 20 日至 23 日連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度下跌 1.22%
，收於 5,373.09 點，各產業類股全面收黑，顯示市場信心仍不穩定。根據 Reuters
報導，截至 10 月 19 日，2026 年外資已累計淨賣超印尼股票約 48.8 億美元。此外，
印尼證交所 26 日宣布，將引進獨立專家及外國投資人代表參與高持股集中度審查，
並自 11 月起加強調查疑似協調交易，必要時凍結可疑帳戶，以回應 MSCI 對市場制
度的疑慮。
二、 下跌原因分析：
1. 股市：潛在指數剔除與資金外流，持續壓抑市場評價
若印尼股市最終降至「獨立市場」，原則上將不再納入 MSCI 新興市場及邊境市場相關
指數，追蹤相關指數的被動式基金將須依規則調整持股，實際處理方式仍以 MSCI 後
續公告為準。若改降至「邊境市場」，則可能獲得部分邊境市場資金承接，衝擊相對較
小。Reuters 引述多家金融機構估計，印尼股市降至獨立市場可能引發約 30 億至 60

億美元被動資金流出，其中 UBS 估計約 40 億美元，並指出主動型基金另有約 150 億
美元潛在撤資風險，惟實際規模仍視最終分類結果及基金策略而定。
2. 匯率：外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力
印尼股市的降評風險不僅衝擊股市，也可能透過外資撤離增加印尼盾貶值壓力。印尼
盾 10 月 16 日盤中一度貶至 18,070 兌 1 美元，為 7 月下旬以來最弱水準之一，當日
收盤約 18,000。若後續國際資金持續流出，可能進一步增加股匯市波動，對以外幣計
價、且未完全避險的印尼基金及 ETF 形成額外壓力。雖然印尼央行可透過匯市干預及

流動性管理措施穩定金融市場，但在資金外流壓力尚未緩解前，印尼盾仍可能維持偏
弱格局。
3. 基金與 ETF：指數權重下滑，單一市場曝險風險升高
MSCI 相關指數調整已對印尼股票產生實質影響。Reuters 引用 LSEG 資料顯示，印尼
在 MSCI 新興市場指數的權重已由 2026 年初的 1.16%降至 9 月底的 0.43%，相關成
分股亦由年初 18 檔減至 9 檔，反映部分指數資金調整壓力已提前出現。對印尼單一
國家基金及 ETF 而言，主要風險來自股市下跌、資金流出及印尼盾波動；主動式基金
雖可透過選股降低部分影響，但仍難以完全迴避整體市場風險。不同產品實際受到的
影響，仍應依追蹤指數、主要持股及匯率避險安排個別評估。
三、 市場看法：
1. 制度性問題難以速解，降評風險將持續牽動市場：我們認為，此次 MSCI 降評風險並
非單純反映經濟成長放緩或企業獲利不佳，而是涉及市場透明度、交易公平性與監理
執法等結構性問題。尤其 MSCI 不僅啟動降評諮詢，更持續限制印尼股票的部分指數

調整，包括不提高外資納入因子、不新增相關成分股，以及不進行向上規模分類調整
，顯示市場可投資性疑慮尚未獲得有效解決。儘管政府試圖推動改革，但制度修正仍
須經過執行與成效驗證，才能重新取得國際投資人的信任，因此短期內降評風險仍難
完全消除。
2. 外資撤離與指數權重下降，市場信心修復恐需較長時間：印尼今年以來外資已大幅賣
超，MSCI 新興市場指數中的權重亦明顯下降，顯示國際投資人對當地市場的疑慮已
逐漸反映於實際資金流向。我們認為，即使印尼最終成功避免降評，部分國際機構投
資人仍可能因市場透明度、流動性及治理品質疑慮而維持較低曝險。此外，MSCI 對
部分指數調整的凍結措施尚未解除，短期內新增指數資金流入機會仍受到限制，預期
國際資金回流與市場評價修復均可能需要較長時間。
3. 監理改革力度加大，但尚不足以扭轉保守展望：印尼政府近期持續加強改革，包括提
高資訊透明度、強化交易監控、對違規交易開罰，以及引進外部獨立人士參與監理審
查。10 月 26 日該國政府進一步提出主動調查疑似協調交易及凍結可疑帳戶等措施，

11 月 2 日起亦規劃將高持股集中度股票排除於雅加達綜合指數之外。相關改革有助改

策，應審慎衡量本身之需求及投資風險，自行承擔一切投資風險並自負盈虧，本行不負任何法律責任。本報告受中華民國著作權法保護為元
大銀行所有，非經本行同意，不得將本報告內容之一部或全部加以複製、轉載或散布。本報告所載述的意見本行可隨時予以更改或撤回，恕
不另行通知。
善交易秩序及短期市場信心，但部分措施仍待正式實施，且實際成效尚未獲得 MSCI
認可。考量制度性問題改善需要時間，政策利多雖可能帶動短線反彈，但尚不足以改
變對印尼股市中期前景的保守判斷。
4. 股市跌深不代表利空出盡，中期風險報酬吸引力偏低：印尼股市在 10 月 16 日及 19
日大幅修正後，雖曾連續四個交易日反彈，但 26 日再度回落，顯示市場尚未形成穩
定回升趨勢。我們認為，當前股市面臨的不僅是短期賣壓，更包括國際投資人信任下
降及風險溢價提高，即使企業基本面未同步惡化，仍可能因評價下修而承受股價壓力

。因此，短線不排除政策利多或跌深後出現技術性反彈，但在 MSCI 態度、外資流向
與印尼盾走勢明顯改善前，反彈延續性仍有疑慮，印尼股市中期展望維持保守。
四、市場展望：
1. 印尼股市維持保守展望，單一市場風險報酬吸引力不足：考量 MSCI 降評風險尚未解除、
外資撤離壓力持續，以及市場治理改革仍有待驗證，我們認為印尼股市未來數季的風險報
酬吸引力相對有限。即使近期股價明顯回落，仍難認定相關風險已充分反映。相較市場制
度成熟、國際資金流動性較佳的新興市場，印尼股市可能持續面臨較高風險溢價，因此對
單一印尼市場的集中曝險應維持審慎態度。
2. 此事件對新興市場指數與東協指數影響相對有限：根據 MSCI 資料，截至 2026 年 9 月底
，印尼股市在 MSCI Emerging Market 指數與 MSCI AC ASEAN 指數中的權重分別約為
0.43%與 7.55%，因此我們認為印尼潛在降評對相關指數的直接衝擊相對有限。此外，新
興市場各國經濟結構與產業組成有所差異，區域型基金可透過跨國分散降低單一市場風險

。惟後續仍須留意印尼事件對整體新興市場投資信心造成的外溢影響，以及全球貿易與匯
率波動風險。

2026年 11月 20日 全6頁

Indicators Update 
2026年 10月全国消費者物価 
電気代などエネルギーが上昇に転じ、コアCPIは伸び率拡大

経済調査部 エコノミスト 横田 凱

[要約] 
◼ 2026年 10月の全国コア CPI（除く生鮮食品）は前年比＋2.2％と前月から伸び率が拡
大した。資源高が波及し電気代やガス代が大きく上昇したことでエネルギーの前年比
伸び率がプラスに転じた。一方、生鮮食品やエネルギーを除いた全国新コアコア CPI
は同＋2.2％と前月から伸び率が縮小した。コメ価格の下落の影響で非耐久消費財（除
く生鮮食品・エネルギー）の伸びが減速したことが主因だ。 
◼ コア CPIの前年比の動きを財・サービス別に見ると、エネルギーの伸び率がプラスに
転じたほか、耐久消費財の伸び率が拡大した。非耐久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネル
ギー）、サービスの伸び率は縮小した。半耐久消費財の伸び率は横ばいだった。 
◼ 先行きの物価上昇率について、コア CPI上昇率は 26年度末にかけて前年比＋3％超ま
で高まると見込んでいる。原油高が、エネルギーだけでなく、幅広い財・サービスの
価格へ波及するとみられる。賃上げの価格転嫁と併せ、基調的な物価への上昇圧力と
なるだろう。27 年度は、こうした物価上昇圧力は継続する一方で、飲食料品にかかる
消費税率の引き下げにより、コアCPIの上昇率は縮小するとみられる。

26年10月 CPI：エネルギーが再びプラスになり、コア CPI上昇率は拡大 
2026年 10月の全国コア CPI（除く生鮮食品）は前年比＋2.2％と前月から伸び率が拡大した
（図表1）。3-5カ月前の原油価格等を参照する電気代やガス代が大きく上昇し、政策による押し
下げ効果を上回ったことで、エネルギー（9月：同▲0.6％→10月：同＋2.2％）が 3カ月ぶり
にプラスに転じたことが主因だ。なお、政府のエネルギー高対策による押し下げの効果は依然
として大きく、政策要因1を除いたコアCPIの伸び率は当社の試算で同＋3.6％程度である2。 
一方、全国新コアコア CPI（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）（前年比＋2.2％）の伸び率は前月
から縮小した。コメ価格の下落などにより、生鮮食品を除く食料の伸びが減速したことで、非
耐久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）の伸び率が縮小した。

小・中学校の給食費無償化、水道料減免を指す。

原油高が反映され電気代や都市ガス代が上昇し、エネルギーの伸び率がプラスに転換 
コア CPIの前年比の動きを財・サービス別に見ると（図表 3）、エネルギー（前年比＋2.2％）
の伸び率がプラスに転じたほか、耐久消費財（同＋4.1％）の伸び率が拡大した。一方、非耐
久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）（同＋2.8％）やサービス（同＋1.4％）の伸び率が縮
小した。また、半耐久消費財（同＋3.1％）の伸び率は前月から横ばいだった。 
エネルギーに含まれる 5品目はいずれも前月から伸び率が拡大した。とりわけ、11カ月ぶり
にプラスに転じた電気代（9月：前年比▲2.4％→10月：同＋1.6％）や、都市ガス代（9月：
同＋0.2％→10月：同＋5.3％）の寄与度が大きい。それぞれ、政府による補助額は前年同月よ
り多かったものの、今年夏ごろの資源高を反映した価格の上昇がそれを上回った。 
耐久消費財では、携帯電話機（9月：前年比＋10.9％→10月：同＋17.2％）やパソコン（ノ
ート型）（9月：同＋0.8％→10月：同＋2.7％）など、電子機器の伸び率が拡大した。 
一方、非耐久消費財（除く生鮮食品、エネルギー）では、うるち米Ｂ（9 月：前年比▲
21.1％→10月：同▲29.8％）やうるち米Ａ（9月：同▲19.6％→10月：同▲29.4％）など、コ
メ価格を中心に一部の食料品の下落幅が拡大した。 
サービスでは、通信料（携帯電話）（9月：前年比＋1.6％→10月：同＋0.4％）や自動車保険
料（任意）（9月：同＋5.9％→10月：同＋4.6％）などで、前年同月の値上げの裏の影響が表れ
たことなどから伸び率が縮小した。 
また、半耐久消費財では、マット（9月：前年比▲0.0％→10月：同＋3.5％）や女性用セー
ター（長袖）（9月：同▲0.2％→10月：同＋2.7％）などの伸び率がプラスに転じた一方、自動
車タイヤ（9月：同＋1.4％→10 月：同＋0.4％）やメモリーカード（9月：同＋152.0％→10
月：同＋107.9％）などの伸び率が縮小した。

1

4 / 6

先行き：原油高などの波及で、コア CPIは26年度末にかけて＋3％超を予想 
先行きの物価上昇率について、コア CPI上昇率は 26年度末にかけて前年比＋3％超まで高ま
るとみている。当面は、原油高の幅広い財・サービスへの波及が物価を押し上げる主因となる
だろう。また、賃金と物価の循環的な上昇も続くとみられる。 
原油価格の動向には注意が必要だ。米国・イスラエルとイランの間で断続的な攻撃の応酬は
続いており、中東の和平に向けた先行きはいまだ不透明である。原油価格の代表的な指標であ
る WTI先物価格は 11月 19日終値で 83ドル/バレル程度と、中東情勢の悪化前よりも高い状況
が続いている。 
足元の原油高は、直接的にエネルギー価格を押し上げる。電気・ガスの燃料費調整制度で
は、3-5カ月前のエネルギー資源の平均輸入価格が料金に反映されるため、原油高がラグを伴
って波及し、前年比での影響は長く続く。関連して、政府は 26年 12月から 27年2月使用分
（請求は翌月）に電気・ガス料金支援を再開する4。しかし、26年1-3月使用分にも補助を行っ
ていたため、前年比でみた物価上昇率の抑制効果は限定的なものとなるだろう（巻末図表「エ
ネルギー価格のコアCPIへの影響とその見通し」）。 
また、原油高は、エネルギー価格だけでなく、輸送費や原材料費の増加を通じて幅広い品目
の価格にも波及するため5、基調的な物価への上昇圧力となるとみられる。さらに、ドル円レー
トは足元で 150円/ドル台後半から 160円/ドル程度と、前年比で円安水準にある。原油価格の
上昇と併せて輸入物価への押し上げ圧力が強い状態であり、消費者物価への波及が見込まれ
る。 
賃上げによる人件費の増加分を販売価格に転嫁する動きも継続するだろう。26年度春闘にお
ける定期昇給相当込みの賃上げ率は、加重平均で5.01％と高水準を維持している6。さらに、26
年度の最低賃金の全国加重平均額も前年度比 5％程度引き上げられた7。こうした賃金上昇は、
サービス価格を中心に物価を押し上げると見込んでいる。 
これらの物価上昇圧力は27年度にかけても継続するだろう。他方、国会で審議中の飲食料
品の消費減税8が法案通り実施されると、コア CPI上昇率は前年比1.2％pt程度押し下げられる
ことから、27年度後半には前年比＋1％台まで縮小するだろう。

4 詳細は資源エネルギー庁「電気・ガス料金支援」（2026年11月20日閲覧）を参照。 
5 中村華奈子「原油高の国内への波及経路と価格転嫁率を踏まえた消費者物価への影響」（大和総研レポー
ト、2026年4月17日） 
6 日本労働組合総連合会（連合）「3年連続の 5％超え！ ～2026春季生活闘争 第7回（最終）回答集計結
果について～」（2026年7月3日） 
7 厚生労働省「令和 8年度 地域別最低賃金 答申状況」（2026年9月3日） 
8 国会で審議中の「租税及び社会保険料による負担の軽減のための飲食料品に係る消費税率の臨時的な引下げ
及び就業者負担軽減支援金の支給に関する法律」が成立すれば、軽減税率対象の飲食料品の消費税率は 2027
年 4月1日から2年間、現在の 8％から 1％へと引き下げられる。

## Document 084: 2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年・冬のボーナス予測 ～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-11-06
Pages: 2
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026 年 11 月 6 日

1/2 
2026 年・冬のボーナス予測 
～前年比＋3.0％と６年連続の増加を予想～ 
 経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

○ 民間企業の2026 年冬のボーナス一人当たり支給額を前年比＋3.0％（昨冬同＋2.8％）と予想する
（厚生労働省「毎月勤労統計」ベース）。冬のボーナスとしては６年連続の増加となるだろう。中
東情勢の悪化に伴う資源価格上昇が企業活動の重荷となるなかでも企業収益は高水準を維持してい
る。人手不足を背景とした処遇改善の動きも根強く、今冬もボーナスの増加が続くと予想する。

○ 11 月６日に厚生労働省から公表された26 年夏のボーナス（賞与支給事業所における労働者一人平
均賞与額）は、前年比＋5.3％と５年連続で増加し、昨夏の同＋2.9％から伸びも拡大した。また、
賞与を支給していない事業所も含む「全事業所における労働者一人平均賞与額」で見ても同＋
5.9％と増加している。賞与を支給する事業所の割合も前年の 73.4％から74.0％へ上昇しており、
企業の従業員への還元姿勢が強いことがうかがえる。

○ ボーナスの交渉では、春闘時にその年の年間賞与を決定する夏冬型が採用されていることが多い。
2026 年春闘では、月例給与だけでなく一時金でも増額回答が目立った。企業収益が高水準にある
ことに加え、物価高への配慮や人材確保の観点から、賞与を含めた処遇改善を進める企業が多かっ
たとみられる。また、月例給与の引き上げが賞与の算定基礎額を押し上げ、支給月数の増加と相ま
ってボーナス増につながる面もある。夏のボーナス増に続き、冬についてもこうした交渉結果が反
映される形で増加が予想される。

○ 毎期型の企業、あるいは労働組合が存在しない中小・零細企業においては、より直近の収益状況・
業況がボーナスに反映されやすい。もっとも、イラン情勢悪化によるコスト上昇が企業収益の下押
し要因となるなかでも、価格転嫁の進展などを背景として企業収益は総じて底堅く推移している。
利益の水準も高く、従業員への還元余力はなお保たれているとみてよいだろう。

○ 人手不足感が強いことも、ボーナス増加を後押しする。企業にとって人材の確保・定着は引き続き
重要な経営課題となっており、月例給与だけでなく賞与を含めた処遇改善の必要性は高い。物価高
によって家計負担が増加するなか、従業員への還元を求める声も強い。こうした点も今冬のボーナ
ス増加を支える要因となる。

○ 労務行政研究所が９月30 日に公表した調査によれば、東証プライム上場企業 175 社の 2026 年年末

### Page 2

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
2/2 
一時金妥結額は、前年比＋4.2％と５年連続で増加した。製造業が同＋4.3％、非製造業が同＋
3.5％と、いずれもプラスとなっている。大企業中心の調査であり、中小企業やパートタイム労働
者も含む毎月勤労統計とは対象が異なるものの、冬のボーナスも堅調に増加するという見方を裏付
ける結果といえる。

○ 冬のボーナス増加は家計の所得環境にとって好材料であり、個人消費を下支えすることが期待され
る。一方で懸念されるのが物価上昇の影響である。中東情勢悪化に伴うコスト上昇の価格転嫁など
を背景として、今後は物価上昇率が高まる可能性が高く、政府による電気・ガス代支援の効果を織
り込んでも、2027 年初にはＣＰＩコアの前年比上昇率が＋３％台に達することが予想される。物
価上昇による家計の購買力の圧迫が続くなか、ボーナス増加による個人消費の押し上げ効果につい
ては過度な期待は禁物だろう。

## Document 088: 2026～2027年度日本経済見通し（2026年11月）（2026年7-9月期GDP1次速報後改定）

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026～2027年度日本経済見通し（2026年11月）（2026年7-9月期GDP1次速報後改定）.pdf

### Page 1

Economic Trends

2026年11月 16日

1/5 
2026～2027年度日本経済見通し（2026年 11月） 
（2026年 7-9月期 GDP1次速報後改定） 
経済調査部 シニアエグゼクティブエコノミスト  新家 義貴 
（℡：050-5474-7490）

日本・国内総生産（ＧＤＰ）成長率予測値

2026年度予測 実質＋０．９％ （前回 ＋１．０％） 
 名目＋３．６％ （前回 ＋３．２％）

2027年度予測 実質＋１．２％ （前回 ＋１．２％） 
 名目＋３．４％ （前回 ＋３．０％）

※前回は 2026年９月８日時点の弊社予測値

○ 実質ＧＤＰ成長率の見通しは 2026年度が＋0.9％（９月時点予測：＋1.0％）、27年度が＋1.2％
（同＋1.2％）である。暦年では 26年が＋0.9％（同＋0.9％）、27年が＋1.0％（同＋1.0％）とな
る。26年７-９月期のＧＤＰ成長率が前回予測時点での想定を下回ったことを反映し、26年度の
成長率見通しを若干下方修正したが、先行きの景気見通しに大きな変更はない。26年度後半は物
価上昇率の再加速が景気の重荷となるものの、企業部門の底堅さが下支えとなり、緩やかな回復
基調は維持されると予想する。また、27年度にはコスト上昇圧力が徐々に和らぐことに加え、食
料品の消費税減税による押し上げもあり、景気の回復感が徐々に強まるとみている。

○ 今回の予測では、2027年４月から２年間、飲食料品にかかる消費税率を１％に引き下げ、あわせ
て中低所得の就業者を対象に年間 6000億円程度の就業者負担軽減支援金を支給することを前提と
している。家計の購買力増大に伴って、27年度の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は0.2％ポイント強押し上げ
られると想定している。また、27年度の消費者物価指数（総合）の前年比上昇率は▲1.4％ポイン
ト、消費者物価指数（生鮮除く総合）は▲1.2％ポイント程度押し下げられるとみられる。

○ 26年７-９月期の実質ＧＤＰ成長率は前期比年率▲0.1％と、４四半期ぶりのマイナス成長となっ
た。個人消費が減少したほか、住宅投資や公共投資も成長率を押し下げた。ただし、個人消費の
減少には、４-６月期にみられた自動車やエアコンなどの耐久財需要の反動や、８～９月の天候不

### Page 2

2/5 
順といった一時的な要因が大きく影響したとみられる。一方、設備投資は高い伸びとなり、企業
部門の底堅さが確認された。前期まで３四半期連続で比較的高い成長が続いていたことも踏まえ
れば、今回の小幅なマイナス成長を悲観視する必要はない。家計と企業の回復に濃淡はみられる
ものの、日本経済は均してみれば緩やかな回復基調にあると評価できる。

○ 26年10-12月期は前期比年率＋0.6％と、再びプラス成長に戻ると予想する。７-９月期に個人消
費を押し下げた耐久財需要の反動や天候不順といった一時的な要因が和らぐことに加え、名目賃
金の高い伸びや雇用の改善が消費を下支えするとみられる。設備投資についても、高水準の企業
収益や人手不足を背景とした省力化・デジタル関連の投資需要は根強く、増加基調を維持するだ
ろう。輸出も、海外景気の底堅さや世界的なＡＩ関連需要の拡大を背景に、緩やかな増加が期待
できる。個人消費は力強さを欠くものの、設備投資や輸出が下支えとなることで、景気の緩やか
な回復基調は維持されるだろう。

○ 26年度後半については、既往の資源高や原材料費、物流費などの上昇が時間差を伴って川下へ波
及することが、景気の下押し要因となる。食料品や日用品、耐久財など幅広い品目への価格転嫁
が進むことに加え、ガソリン補助金の段階的縮小も物価を押し上げる。こうした物価上昇率の再
加速によって、足元で続いている実質賃金の改善が一服し、家計の実質購買力が再び圧迫される
可能性がある。また、企業にとっても原材料費や物流費などの上昇が収益の重荷となるだろう。

○ もっとも、こうしたコスト上昇による下押しに対して、高水準の企業収益が一定の緩衝材となる
ことに加え、ＡＩ関連需要の拡大が半導体製造装置や電子部品などの輸出・生産、設備投資を押
し上げていることは景気の下支え要因となる。人手不足を背景とした省力化投資やデジタル関連
投資への需要も根強く、設備投資は増加基調を維持するとみられる。また、海外景気の底堅さも
輸出を支えるだろう。個人消費についても、物価高による下押し圧力が強まるものの、高い賃上
げや底堅い雇用環境が支えとなり、腰折れする可能性は低い。家計部門は力強さを欠く一方、企
業部門は堅調さを維持しており、日本経済は緩やかな回復基調を続けると予想する。

○ 27年度については、原油高や原材料費上昇による物価上昇圧力が徐々に減衰することで、景気の
回復感は次第に強まるとみている。26年秋の原油高の影響が時間差を伴って波及するため、年度
前半は物価上昇圧力がなお残るものの、年度後半にかけて価格転嫁の動きは徐々に落ち着くと予
想する。物価上昇率の鈍化によって家計の実質購買力が改善し、高い賃上げもあいまって、個人
消費の回復力が強まるだろう。食料品への消費税減税と就業者負担軽減支援金の支給も消費を押
し上げる要因となる。企業部門では、コスト上昇圧力の緩和によって収益環境が改善することも
支えとなり、省力化やデジタル・ＡＩ関連を中心とした設備投資が増加する見込みである。輸出
についても、海外景気の底堅さやＡＩ関連需要が引き続き下支え要因となる。物価面からの逆風
が和らぐことで、家計と企業の双方で内需の持ち直しが進み、27年度の景気回復の勢いは 26年度

### Page 3

3/5 
よりも強まると予想する。

○ 先行きのリスクとして注意が必要なのは、米国を中心としたＡＩ関連投資の持続性である。米中
間選挙後、州・地方政府によるデータセンター建設への規制強化が進めば、計画の延期や投資コ
ストの増加を通じて、ＡＩ関連投資の拡大ペースが鈍化する可能性がある。加えて、巨額投資の
収益性や資金調達の持続性に対する懸念も強まっている。これまでの米国景気はＡＩ投資の拡大
と株高に支えられてきた面が大きいため、仮にＡＩブームに変調が生じれば、その影響はハイテ
ク業界にとどまらず、米国経済、ひいては世界経済に波及する恐れがある。ＡＩ関連投資が大幅
に減速すれば、日本の半導体製造装置や電子部品などの輸出・生産、設備投資にも悪影響が及ぶ
だろう。もっとも、建設規制の強化とＡＩ需要そのものの減少は別問題である。ＡＩの活用拡大
に伴う計算需要は引き続き極めて強く、大手テクノロジー企業の投資意欲も旺盛である。既に着
工済みのデータセンターや契約済みの設備投資案件も多く、規制の影響を受けて投資先の地域を
変更する動きも考えられる。ＡＩ関連投資の拡大ペースは幾分鈍化する可能性があるものの、失
速するとは考えにくく、先行きも高水準の投資が続くと予想している。

○ 消費者物価指数（生鮮食品除く総合、ＣＰＩコア）は 26年度が＋2.3％（９月時点予測：＋
2.4％）、27年度が＋1.3％（同＋1.1％）と予想する。26年９月のＣＰＩコアは前年比＋2.1％と
９カ月ぶりの＋２％台となったが、年度後半にかけて一段と上昇率が高まる可能性が高い。食料
品価格は足元で鈍化傾向に歯止めがかかりつつあり、日用品でも値上げの動きが明確化してい
る。26年春の資源価格上昇や調達難の影響に加え、包装資材費や物流費などのコスト増が時間差
を伴って川下へ転嫁されることが物価を押し上げる。ＡＩ需要の拡大や円安の影響による耐久財
価格の上昇も続くとみられる。また、既往の燃料価格上昇は電気・ガス料金に波及するほか、ガ
ソリン補助金の段階的縮小も押し上げ要因となる。なお、冬場の電気・ガス料金補助は一定の押
し下げ要因となるが、前年比への影響は限定的とみられる。企業の価格転嫁姿勢が積極化してい
ることもあり、27年初にかけてＣＰＩコアは前年比＋３％を上回る可能性が高い。

○ 27年度については、26年秋の原油高の影響が時間差を伴って川下へ波及するため、年度前半は物
価上昇圧力が根強く残るとみられる。もっとも、原油高や原材料費上昇によるコストプッシュ圧
力は次第に弱まり、消費税要因を除いても、年度後半にかけて物価上昇率は緩やかに鈍化してい
くと予想する。食料品や日用品、耐久財などへの価格転嫁の動きも次第に落ち着き、物価面から
の家計への下押し圧力が和らぐことで、個人消費の持ち直しが後押しされるだろう。一方、高い
賃上げに伴う人件費上昇や人手不足、企業の積極的な価格設定姿勢は続くとみられ、サービス価
格を中心とした基調的な物価上昇圧力は維持されるとみている。なお、今回の予測では、27年４
月から食料品の消費税率を８％から１％に引き下げることを前提としており、これにより 27年度
のＣＰＩコア上昇率が約1.2％ポイント押し下げられると想定している。

### Page 4

4/5 
○ 金融政策について、日銀は 26年12月の金融政策決定会合で政策金利を1.25％から1.50％に引き
上げると予想する。日銀は 10月会合で、基調的な物価上昇率が２％目標と概ね整合的な水準に達
したとの判断を示し、金融政策運営の重点は「２％への到達」から「２％での定着」へと移っ
た。今後は基調的な物価上昇率を２％程度で安定させることが重要になるが、物価上振れリスク
が残るなか、現在の金融環境はなお緩和的であり、日銀は中立金利に向けて金融緩和の度合いを
縮小していくと考えられる。

○ その後も27年３月に1.75％、６月に2.00％へと、３カ月程度の間隔で追加利上げを行うと予想
する。27年度にはコスト要因による物価上昇圧力が徐々に弱まるとみられるものの、賃金上昇や
人手不足を背景とした基調的な物価上昇圧力は維持されるだろう。物価上振れへの警戒が残るな
か、日銀は段階的な利上げを継続すると予想する。なお、今回の利上げ局面の最終到達点（ター
ミナルレート）は2.0％と見込むが、中立金利の水準には大きな不確実性がある。基調的な物価上
昇率が想定以上に上振れれば 2.0％を超える利上げもあり得るだろう。一方、景気の下振れや既往
の利上げの累積的な影響が想定以上に強まる場合には、利上げペースが鈍化する可能性もある。

Update
TSMC  | Asia Pacific
Asia Pacific Summit 2026
Feedback
AI demand and key strategy: TSMC remains confident in the AI mega trend.
Technology roadmap and capex capacity planning are the most critical decisions for
company's long-term development.
US power constraint: Regarding the US power infrastructure shortage concerns,
TSMC has its own internal assessment process and disciplines, and combining with
customers' feedback, there is no change in the demand forecast.

Capacity and US expansion: TSMC is still aiming to more than double its 2nm
capacity by next year, given strong demand from AI and smartphone. The company
also continues to evaluate the next US fab site, and there was a discussion with
Tesla for TeraFab cooperation in parallel.
GPU and ASIC customers: TSMC will continue support major GPU customers, as it
does with other customers' needs. Both GPU and ASIC are TSMC's customers, and
TSMC have been balancing customers' needs.

Pricing and margin: TSMC's pricing reflects its value and cost inflation. Although
semiconductor and foundry remain a cyclical industry, TSMC hopes its margin will
be consistent and sustainable through the cycle.

STOCK RATINGS
Morgan Stanley uses a relative rating system using terms such as Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated or Underweight (see definitions below). Morgan Stanley does not assign ratings of Buy,
Hold or Sell to the stocks we cover. Overweight, Equal-weight, Not-Rated and Underweight are not the equivalent of buy, hold and sell.  Investors should carefully read the definitions of all
ratings used in Morgan Stanley Research. In addition, since Morgan Stanley Research contains more complete information concerning the analyst's views, investors should carefully read Morgan

Stanley Research, in its entirety, and not infer the contents from the rating alone.  In any case, ratings (or research) should not be used or relied upon as investment advice.  An investor's decision
to buy or sell a stock should depend on individual circumstances (such as the investor's existing holdings) and other considerations.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.
Unless otherwise specified, the time frame for price targets included in Morgan Stanley Research is 12 to 18 months.
Analyst Industry Views
Attractive (A): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be attractive vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated
below.
In-Line (I): The analyst expects the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months to be in line with the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.
Benchmarks for each region are as follows: North America - S&P 500; Latin America - relevant MSCI country index or MSCI Latin America Index; Europe - MSCI Europe; Japan - TOPIX; Asia -
relevant MSCI country index or MSCI sub-regional index or MSCI AC Asia Pacific ex Japan Index.

廠商，主要經營氟素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相

脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段

的企業。3Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設
備營收占 69.7% ， 氟 素 內 襯 管 件 類 占
10.2%，氟素應用材料類占 16.5 %，氟素

主要競爭對手：

上品 (4770 TT) 
在手訂單金額提升，持續擴充產能因應

永豐觀點 
上品在手訂單金額提升至 80 億元，隨著先前低毛利訂單陸續消化完畢，產品組合
預期改善，公司規劃於 2027 持續進行產能擴充，後市展望持續正向，研究部維持
正向看待上品獲利表現。 
 投資評價與建議 
投資建議維持買進，主要係因：上品在手訂單金額持續提升至 80 億元，反映台灣
及中國地區接單表現持續暢旺，隨著公司低毛利訂單出貨完畢，產品組合應逐步好
轉，且公司規劃於 2027 持續擴充產能，挹注未來營收動能，研究部預估公司
2026/2027 稅後 EPS 14.55/23.01 元，參考歷史 PER 區間約位於 10-20X，以
2027 年預估 EPS，給予 15X PER 評價，目標價 345 元，投資建議維持買進。

ESG 評析 
上品企業永續評鑑整體為 B+等，於 SASB 產業永續議題與跨產業五大永續面向平
均得分分別為 B+等與 B+等。 
SinoPac+ ESG 評鑑系統評等

專業意見，對於評分結果及評估報告內容之完整性及真實性，不負擔保責任，亦不對閱讀或使用
本評估報告之第三方負任何責任。

### Page 2

2

上品為從事氟素樹脂材料相關製品之生產廠商：公司成立於 1981 年，主要經營氟
素樹脂材料（鐵氟龍）相關製品，包括氟素原料加工之半成品及成品生產及銷售，
下游應用廣泛，是全球氟素樹脂加工應用廠商中少數能同時滿足各階段設備、製
造、研發、創新、設計等完整服務的企業。3Q26 產品營收比重，氟素內襯設備營
收占 69.7%，氟素內襯管件類占 10.2%，氟素應用材料類占 16.5 %，氟素工程收
入占 3.7%；3Q26 公司產品依應用類別區分，半導體占營收 95.1%，化工應用占
比為 3.1%，面板及太陽能應用占 1.4%，其他應用占 0.4%，3Q26 公司營收按地
區別區分，台灣占 47.4%，中國占 48.2%，美國占 0.5%，其他亞洲占 1.6%，歐
洲占 2.2%。 
3Q26 EPS 4.27 元，優於預期：上品 3Q26 營收 16 億元（+22.5% QoQ，+14.4% 
YoY），優於預期，主因上品於 2Q26 擴產，並持續拉升產能利用率，推升營收表
現，毛利率 34.64%（-1.91pts. QoQ，+4.91pts. YoY），低於預期，主因上品出
貨較多低毛利訂單，影響產品組合所致，稅後淨利 34.1 億元（+37.2% QoQ，
+39.9% YoY），優於預期，其中，營業費用低於預期，主因認列預期信用損失金
額降低所致，3Q26 稅後 EPS 4.27 元，優於預期。

### Page 3

3

在手訂單金額持續提升：上品在手訂單金額達 80 億元，較前次發報告時 75 億元
持續提升，其中，約 75%為彰濱廠訂單，約 25%為嘉興廠訂單，預期到年底在手
訂單金額將持續再提升。2026 接單槽車數量達 1,100 台，反映台灣化學品廠因應
晶圓廠大客戶需求而擴充產能，對於上品內襯設備及內襯管件需求同步增溫，而中
國記憶體廠及化學品廠亦持續擴充，下單動能有所增溫。隨著市況於 2026 大幅改
善，彰濱廠及嘉興廠已可挑選毛利率較佳的訂單進行承接，產品組合預期將逐步好
轉，有利其毛利率逐步回升。 
2027 上品計畫持續擴充產能：因應客戶需求強勁，公司於 2Q26 進行擴產，並持
續招工補充人力，拉升產能利用率，目前彰濱廠及嘉興廠已達近滿載水準，公司年
初彰濱廠槽車約當產能約為月產能 80 台，近期已提升至月產能 130 台，預期年底
將達滿載規模月產能 140 台，公司規劃將於 6M27 再擴充至月產能 180 台，嘉興
廠亦規劃提升產能約 30%，以因應客戶強勁需求。而先前承接的低毛利訂單仍有
部分尚未出貨完畢，預期將於 1Q27 出貨完畢，因營收比重持續下降，預期產品組
合應持續改善，有利毛利率及獲利表現回升。

後市展望正向，投資建議維持買進：展望後市，台灣化學品廠及中國記憶體、化學
品廠擴建，推升對上品氟素樹脂產品需求，上品在手訂單金額持續提升，隨著上品
先前承接低毛利訂單陸續出貨完畢，產品組合應逐步改善，推升毛利率及獲利表現

投資建議。

### Page 1

AI 高階測試需求維持強勁，成長趨勢不變 
◼ 產品組合有望優化，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元：CPC 及 VPC
稼動率皆維持高檔，預估 4Q26 營收 63.95 億元(+66.7%YoY)。獲利
方面，受惠產品組合持續優化，預估毛利率季增至 61.92%，隨營收
規模持續擴大，費用率有望年減至 25%，營益率 37.12%，稅後淨
利 20.75 億元(+119%YoY)，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元。 
◼ 產能擴充計畫持續推進，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 68.44 元：展望後
市，隨 AI HPC 需求持續成長，探針卡朝高頻高速、大電流及高針腳
數方向發展，帶動高階測試需求維持強勁。為解決產能瓶頸，旺矽
將積極擴充產能，2026/2027 年底 MEMS 月產能為 350 萬/600 萬
針，VPC 月產能為 200 萬/250 萬針，且 PCB 廠房預計 2H27 投產，
有助提升 PCB 自製率。整體而言，預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 
68.44/118.84 元，且 CPO 相關設備有望於 2028 年較明顯挹注營
收，預估 2028 年稅後 EPS 204.56 元。 
◼ 維持買進評等，目標價 6,500 元：評價方面，以 2028 年 EPS 估算，
目前 PE 約為 21X，低於旺矽近 2 年 30-55X，考量 AI 高階測試需求
持續強勁，且 CPO 布局有望挹注中長期成長動能，長線維持買進評
等，目標價調整為 6,500 元(2028F PE 32X)。

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓 (02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號         Page 2 of 6 
旺矽為全球前五大探針卡廠商  旺矽 1995 年 7 月於新竹成立，實收資本額 9.8 億元，截至 Apr-26，員工人數
共 2,110 人。主要業務包含晶圓探針卡測試及半導體設備銷售。旺矽提供一站
式購足服務，探針卡業務包含懸臂式探針卡(Cantilever Probe Cards)、垂直式
探針卡(Vertical Probe Cards)及微機電探針卡(MEMS)等；半導體設備則例如
光電自動化設備(PA)、溫控測試設備(Thermal)、先進半導體測試設備
(Advanced Semiconductor Test, AST)等。以 3Q26 產品別營收結構而言，探
針卡 72.7%，設備 25.4%，其他 1.9%。

Exhibit 3：營收佔比別預估

Exhibit 4：旺矽探針卡全球排名

3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.18 元，優於
預期

受惠 AI HPC 高階測試需求強勁，旺矽 3Q26 營收 59.04 億元(+12.8%QoQ，
+72.9%YoY)。獲利方面，由於產品組合優化，毛利率季增至 61.68%，且因費
用率較原先預期低，營業利益 22.76 億元(+26.3%QoQ，+154.1%YoY)。業外
認列匯兌利益後，稅後淨利 18.8 億元(+23.3%QoQ，+114.4%YoY)，3Q26 稅
後 EPS 19.18 元，優於原先預估之 18.07 元。

台新證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市仁愛路四段 118 號 16 樓 (02)5589-9558  (113)金管投顧新字第 021 號         Page 3 of 6 
產品組合有望優化，預估 4Q26
稅後 EPS 21.18 元 
 由於 AI HPC 客 戶 拉 貨 動 能 持 續 強 勁 ， 旺 矽 Oct-26 營收 22.32 億元
(+8.98%MoM，+92.73%YoY)，優於預期。考量 CPC 及 VPC 稼動率皆維持高
檔，預估 4Q26 營收 63.95 億元(+8.3%QoQ，+66.7%YoY)。獲利方面，受惠
產品組合持續優化，預估毛利率季增至 61.92%，隨營收規模持續擴大，費用
率有望年減至 25%，營益率 37.12%，稅後淨利 20.75 億元(+10.4%QoQ，
+119%YoY)，預估 4Q26 稅後 EPS 21.18 元。

產能擴充計畫持續推進，預 估
2026 年稅後 EPS 68.44 元

展望後市，隨 AI 與 HPC 需求持續成長，探針卡朝高頻高速、大電流及高針腳
數方向發展，帶動高階測試需求維持強勁。為解決產能瓶頸，旺矽將積極擴充
產能，包含：MEMS 月產能自 2025 年底 100 萬針，預期 2026 年底提升至 350
萬針，2027 年底目標為 600 萬針；VPC 月產能則自 2025 年底 120 萬針，預
期 2026 年底提升為 200 萬針，2027 年底有望再增加至 250 萬針；CPC 月產
能則維持持平略增。廠房方面，旺矽 Nov-26 中旬公告投入 22 億元進行湖口廠
機電工程，PCB 廠房預計如期於 2H27 投產，有助提升 PCB 自製率；另，
CPO 設備所需廠房改建，預計 1Q27 完成。資本支出方面，預估 2026/2027 年
分別為 15-18 億元及 20 億元，2027 年主要用於設備採購。

整體而言，投顧預估 2026 年營收 214.7 億元(+60.5%YoY)，受惠產品組合持
續優化，毛利率 60.5%，營益率 36%，稅後淨利 67.1 億元(+111.1%YoY)，上
修 2026 年稅後 EPS 至 68.44 元(原預估 64.97 元)。

Exhibit 6：旺矽擴產時程推估

Sources: Company，Taishin 
高階測試需求續強，預估 2027
年稅後 EPS 118.84 元 
 旺矽自 2026 年起整併 LED 與 AST 部門，並持續布局 CPO 檢測專案，目前主
要聚焦於 CPO insertion 2 之 EIC/PIC 測試及 insertion 3 之 die-level 測試應
用。由於 CPO 測試涉及光電整合，技術門檻及客製化程度較高，目前仍持續
與客戶進行驗證及開發。其中，insertion 2 既有客戶預計 2026 年底更新驗證
進度，另有新客戶持續接洽中，相關進展有望於 1H27 較為明朗；insertion 3
則已有多家國際客戶出貨工程機，且持續交機中，有望 1H27 逐步轉為量產機
需求。展望後市，隨 AI 高速傳輸需求提升，CPO 相關測試設備有望成為 AST
業務中長期成長動能，惟考量設備交期較長及客戶驗收認證所需時間，預期
2028 年營收貢獻將較為顯著，CPO 相關業務營收占比有望達雙位數。

展望 2027 年，隨新增產能逐步開出及新廠投入營運，且客戶組成較為多元，
市占率有望持續提升，惟考量 CPO 設備交期及客戶驗收時程較長，預估 2027
年營收 355.6 億元(+65.7%YoY)。獲利方面，隨高頻高速測試需求增加，產品
組合有望持續優化，預估毛利率 62.2%，稅後淨利 116.4 億元(+73.6%YoY)，
調整 2027 年稅後 EPS 至 118.84 元(原預估 122.27 元)。展望 2028 年，隨探
針及 PCB 產能持續擴增，且 AST 部門營收貢獻提升，上修 2028 年稅後 EPS 
204.56 元(原預估 196.39 元)。

維持買進評等，目標價 6,500 元

評價方面，以 2028 年 EPS 估算，目前 PE 約為 21X，低於旺矽近 2 年 30-
55X，考量 AI 高階測試需求持續強勁，且 CPO 布局有望挹注中長期成長動
能，長線維持買進評等，目標價調整為 6,500 元(2028F PE 32X)。

風險提示  1) 客戶拉貨不如預期；2) 新品研發不如預期；3) 匯率變化。

2026.10.30   
현대차 (005380)

26.3Q Review: 이제 올라가야 할 때 Company Brief

본업 Review: 26.3Q 매출액 45.1조원, 영업이익 2.6조원  
현대차는 26.3Q 매출액 45.1원 (-3.4% YoY, -8.3% QoQ), 영업이익 2.6조원 (+5.5% 
YoY, -6.1% QoQ, +5.9% OPM) 등의 실적을 시현했다 [iM증권 추정치 매출액: 
45.3조원, 영업이익: 2.4조원]. 매출액은 시장 컨센서스에 부합했고, 영업이익의 경우 
시장 컨센서스였던 2.4조원을 소폭 상회했다. 주요 구성 요소 별 내용은 아래와 같다.  
① P: 26.3Q HEV 판매 대수는 16.6만대를 기록하며 전사 판매 대비 비중 
18.2% 달성 (+18.8% YoY, -11.1% QoQ). 미국의 경우 국내 공장 생산 
감소에 따른 수출 부진으로 도매 판매는 소폭 감소했으나, 판매 비중은 
25.2%로 전반적인 믹스는 직전 분기와 유사. 26.3Q 평균환율이 직전 분기 
대비 크게 하락했으나, 전년 동기 기저 탓에 오히려 + 환율 효과 관찰됨. 
② Q: 26.3Q 도매 판매 대수는 91.8만대로 전년 동기 대비 11.5% 감소 (이하 
YoY 기준, 미국: -10.0%, 유럽: -18.1% 국내: -27.2% 인도: +18.3%). 
26.2Q 안전 공업 화재에 이어 26.3Q에 발생한 파업의 영향으로 생산 
대수는 감소. 국내 공장에서의 생산 차질은 내수 판매에 더해 수출 감소, 즉 
전체적인 판매 축소로 연결됨. 26.4Q부터 생산 관련 노이즈는 발생하지 
않을 전망. 이에 더해 아반떼, 투싼 등의 신차 판매 효과를 통한 반등 가능.  
③ C: 26.3Q 관세 비용은 직전 분기와 유사한 수준으로 발생한 것으로 파악 
(25.2Q 0.8조원, 25.3Q 1.8조원, 25.4Q 1.5조원, 26.1Q 0.8조원, 26.2Q 
0.9조원). 이란 사태에 따른 주요 원자재 가격 상승 압력은 계속 존재하나, 
내부 원가 절감 정책으로 약 5,000억원의 원가 상승분을 상쇄. 현대차는 
26.4Q 원가 절감 정책의 효과가 더 크게 나타날 것이라고 언급. 26.3Q 
기말 환율이 전년 동기 대비 급락함에 따라 판매보층충당부채전입액 또한 
감소 (26.3Q 매출원가, 판매관리비 각 전년 동기 대비 1.7%, 19.2% 감소). 
금융 부문의 경우 26.3Q 매출액 9.1조원 (+27.8% YoY), 영업이익 4,710억원 (-
18.2% YoY, -37.9%) 등의 실적을 기록했다. 완성차 판매가 전반적으로 
위축되었음에도 외형 성장을 이어갔으나, 금리 상승에 따른 이자 비용 등 전반적인 
조달 관련 비용이 증가했다. 일부 EV를 중심으로 중고차 가격이 소폭 하락한 점 또한 
HCA 등 주요 부문 이익 감소의 배경이다. 26.3Q 현대차 순이익은 1,932억원을 
기록했는데, 이는 영업외손익이 전년 동기 789억원에서 229억원으로 감소했기 
때문이다. 현대차는 주요 환율 하락에 따른 외화환산손실 증가의 영향이라고 밝혔다. 
1) 생산 단에서의 노이즈가 연속해 발생하며 내수 판매 및 수출 감소가 불가피했으나, 
2) 원자재 가격 상승 영향을 내부 원가 절감 정책을 통해 상쇄했고, 3) 주요 
평균환율이 하락했으나 기말환율 또한 급락하며 판매보증충당부채전입액 감소 효과가 
나타나 시장 기대를 소폭 상회하는 수익성이 관찰되었다는 것이 26.3Q 실적 총평이다. 
현대차는 2026년 도매 판매 가이던스를 416만대에서 400만대로 하향 조정했다. 
26.3Q 누적 도매 판매 대수는 288만대이기에 하향 조정은 불가피했다. 단 신규 
가이던스에 따르면 26.4Q 판매 목표는 약 110만대 수준이라는 것인데, 아반떼, 투싼, 
주요 모델 페이스리프트, GV80 HEV 등의 신차 효과가 얼마나 나타날지가 관건일 
것이다 (이하 분기 도매 판매, 25.1Q 100.1만대, 25.2Q 105.7만대, 25.3Q 103.8만대).

미래 사업 업데이트: 자율주행은 계획대로, Atlas는 조금씩 변신중
26.3Q 컨퍼런스 콜에서 특별히 SDV~Boston Dynamics 관련 업데이트는 확인되지
않았으나 여러 매체를 통해 파악된 현대차그룹의 미래 사업 진행 상황은 다음과 같다.
① SDV/자율주행: 데이터 플라이 휠에 대한 중요성 강조. 27년 말부터 광주
권역 자율주행 실증 사업에 투입되는 차량은 Atria AI 활용. SDV 플랫폼이
적용된 차량 양산 시작은 29년. 양산 SDV 출하량 증가함에 따라 29년부터
GPU 5만개 이상으로 구성되는 새만금 데이터센터를 자체 운영을 목표함.
박민우 포티투닷 사장 겸 AVP 본부장이 부임한 이후 Tesla, nVIDIA 출신의
개발 인력을 충원 중인 상황. 26.05 그랜저 페이스리프트 모델에 플레오스
탑재 시작. 26.09부터 판매되고 있는 신형 아반떼에도 플레오스 탑재 완료.

② Boston Dynamics: 10.8 Rohit Prasad를 CEO로 선임. Rohit Prasad은
아마존에서 Alex 개발에 중추적인 역할을 수행했던 인물. 현대차그룹에서
직접 하드웨어 개발 및 양산을 챙기고, 소프트웨어에 역량을 높이겠다는
행보로 판단. 26.10 Atlas의 신규 덱스트러스를 공개했는데, 기존 제품 대비
자유도가 크게 증가 (7DoF→14DoF). 순수 직구동 방식을 여전히
고수함.10.27 Boston Dynamics가 주요 부품 업체에게 Atlas 1만대 물량의
부품 PO를 곧 발송할 것이라는 기사가 보도됨. 28년까지 연 3만대 CAPA의
공장 완공이라는 기존 계획 대비 양산 및 개발 속도가 빨라진다는 시그널임.
현대차그룹의 미래 사업 업데이트를 또 찾아볼 수 있을 가장 빠른 이벤트는 CES
2027이다. 다만 CES 2027에는 현대차가 아닌 그룹사가 참여함에 따라 해당 행사에서

유의미한 업데이트 내용은 찾아보기 힘들 것이며 이에 중장기 관점의 접근이 필요하다.
시장의 우려가 반영된 주가 하락: 빨라질 속도와 올라올 본업에 주목
현대차 주가는 26.2H 접어들며 크게 하락하고 있고, CES 2026 전후의 상승폭을 사실상
모두 반납했다 (연초 기준 -1.3%, 26.2H 이후 -41.7%). 또한 최근 시장 반등
흐름에서도 사실상 소외되고 있다. 현대차를 바라보는 투자자들의 우려는 아래와 같다.
첫째, CID 전후로 Boston Dynamics 관련 업데이트가 너무 부족하다는 것이다. 26.08
현대차 CID 행사를 앞두고 많은 투자자들은 빅테크 업체들의 지분 투자 및 Atlas
개발과 관련해 새로운 내용을 기대한 것이 사실이다. 이런 상황에서 유럽, 내수 등 주요
지역 에서의 판매 업황 또한 좋지 않다 보니, 시장의 실망은 더욱 컸다. 전술한 것과
같이 CES 2027에 현대차가 참여하지 않는다는 점에서 내년에 대한 기대도 크지 않다.

둘째, 내수와 유럽 업황이 그룹사 대비 너무 좋지 못하다는 점이다. 26.3Q 현대차 내수,
유럽 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 각 27.2%, 18.1% 감소했다. 반면 26.3Q 기아 내수,
유럽 도매 판매는 전년 동기 대비 각 2.2%, 18.7% 증가했다. 특히 유럽에서의 부진은
EV 라인업이 상대적으로 빈약하고, 중국 업체와의 경쟁이 심화되고 있다는 구조적
문제에서 출발하기에, 투자자들은 26년을 넘어 27, 28년의 성장까지 우려하고 있다
단 우리는 Boston Dynamics의 Atlas는 양산이라는 방향성이 설정된지 오래되지
않았다는 점을 기억해야 한다. 초기 단계부터 양산을 위해 개발된 Tesla Optimus 대비
개발 속도가 빠를 수는 없다. 1~2만대 수준의 데모 제품이 확보되고, 이를 바탕으로
데이터 수집이 일정 부분 확보되는 이후부터 관련 사업 확대 속도는 빨라 질것이다.

취약 지역 판매 업황의 경우에도 내수를 시작으로 점진적으로 개선될 것이다. 생산 차질
외에도 Tesla 및 중국 OEM들의 시장 침투가 우려됨은 분명하지만, 주력 차종 중에서도
국내 판매 볼륨이 큰 아반떼와 투싼의 신차 효과는 분명 기대되는 지점이다. 유럽에서의
반등은 시간이 필요할 것이나, 일단 아이오닉 3가 26.4Q부터 본격적으로 판매될
예정이다. 단 이는 큰 변동 없이 IAA가 2028년부터 시행된다는 전제에 근거하고 있다.
현대차 목표주가 600,000원 유지: 여전한 Multiple 할증 요인
현대차 목표주가를 600,000원으로 유지한다. 26년 EPS 기준 6~8배 수준의 P/E
Multiple을 부여받고 있는 Peer Group들은 중국 시장 M/S 축소로 수익성이 급락하고
있고, 미래 사업 경쟁력은 여전히 전무하다. 현대차의 Multiple 할증 요인은 충분하다.

그림16. IAA 법안 개요

자료: iM증권 리서치본부

그림17. 유럽 생산 OEM 생산원가 추이 
 `  
자료: iM증권 리서치본부

IAA는 EU판 IRA로 볼 수 있음. 현대차그룹의 경우 현대차 (터키, 체코)와 기아 (슬로바키아) 모두 유렵 현지
생산 라인을 이미 확보했고, 현대모비스를 중심으로 한 부품 자급률 비중 이미 높음. 또한 주요 부품에 있어
FTA 체결국의 원산지를 EU산과 동일하게 적용하는 규정 존재. 실제 발효까지 큰 변수가 없다면 국내 완성차
업체의 경우 구조적으로 수혜 클 것. 단 중국 업체들에 대한 명시적 배제 규정이 없다는 점은 한계 중 하나. 
IAA 주요 조항 주요 내용
적용 대상
공공조달 차량, 공적 지원(구매 보조금 등)을 받는 차량, 법인차가 대상임. 전기
차, PHEV, 수소차에 적용됨. 2028년부터 회원국은 무공해 및 저공해이면서 EU
산인 차량이 아니면 법인차를 지원할 수 없음. 판매 금지가 아니라 수요 측 혜택
을 역내 생산과 연동하는 구조
역내 부품 요건
1단계(발효 6개월 후)는 EU 조립, 배터리 제외 부품가치 70% EU산, 셀 포함
배터리 핵심부품 3개 EU산을 요구함. 2단계(1단계 3년 후)는 배터리 핵심부품
5개(셀, 양극재,BMS 포함)와 전동 파워트레인·전장 가치 50% EU산이 추가됨.
소형차 특례
전장 4.2m 이하 전기 승용차가 대상임. EU 조립에 더해 부품가치 70%와
배터리 핵심부품 3개 중 하나만 충족하면 됨. 충족 시 CO2 규제상 1.3대로 인정
됨(2025~34년).
FDI 조건 1억 유로 초과 투자가 대상이며, 지분 49% 상한, EU 인력 50% 이상, EU산 투 입재 30% 이상 등의 조건이 붙음.
FTA·관세동맹국 예외
FTA나 관세동맹을 맺은 국가의 부품은 EU산과 동등한 부품으로 인정되며,
정부조달협정(GPA) 가입국도 일부 조항에 포함됨. 단 최종 조립국까지 인정되
는지는 불명확하며, 조문상으로는 EU, EEA 역내 조립만 인정되는 것으로 해석
됨. 따라서 한국산 부품은 EU산으로 인정 가능하나, 한국이나 튀르키예에서 조
립한 완성차는 요건 미충족 가능성이 높음.
FTA 예외 배제 조건
집행위는 위임법으로 특정국을 동등 대상에서 제외할 수 있음. 해당국이 EU 기업
에 내국민 대우를 하지 않는 경우, 공급망 의존도나 공급 안보 위협이 있는 경우,
해당 협정의 예외 조항에 해당하는 경우가 배제 사유임. 중국은 FTA가 없어 애초
에 대상이 아님.
2020 2021 2022 2023
대당 생산원가 30,683 32,029 35,079 38,140
대당 판매가격 34,404 27,557 31,244 33,560
1인당 인건비 70,981 74,904 78,348 88,198
통상적으로 중국 OEM의 생산 원가 (중국 생산 가정)은 글로벌 OEM 대비 30% 낮다고 추정됨. BYD의 경우 돌핀 등과 같
은 C 세그먼트 차량에서의 유럽 수익성이 낮지만, A~B 세그먼트 급의 돌핀 서프의 경우 매우 높은 수익성 시현. 이는 유럽
시장 내의 A~B 세그먼트 경쟁 강도가 낮다는 점에서 기인. 즉 100% 수준의 관세가 부과되지 않는다면 BYD의 가격 경쟁
력은 유지되는 것. 따라서 EU는 2,000유로 수준의 중국 OEM의 대당 이익을 상쇄하기 위해 이에 준하는 수준으로 지급되
는 EV 구매 보조금 및 법인 차량 세제혜택을 검토 중인 것이고, 이를 IAA에 담아낼지가 핵심.
유럽 생산 OEM 생산원가 및 판매가격 추이

그림23. 26.3Q 국내 완성차 실적 Summary

자료: iM증권 리서치본부

현대차 미주: 펠리세이드 리콜 이슈 존재하나, 메타플랜트 및 기존 앨러배마 공
장 HEV 생산을 통한 시장 진출 속도 빨라질 것으로 전망
인도: GM 탈레가온 공장 인수를 통한 생산량 확대에 나설 예정이고, 이
를 통한 인도 내수 수요 대응 및 글로벌 신흥 시장 진출 또한 목표
유럽: 기아 대비 EV 라인업의 폭에 열위에 있으나, 캐스퍼 EV, EV 3를
통해 EV 수요 회복 국면을 앞두고 있는 유럽 시장 대응에 나설 예정
기아 미주: 텔룰라이드 HEV 출시를 통해 Mid-Size  HEV 수요 대응 이뤄지
고 있음. 스포티지 및 셀토스 등 HEV를 통한 기존 차급 대응 강화 전망
인도: 2030년까지 10개 차종 출시 목표하고 있고, 이를 통해 2030년
41만대 도매 판매, 시장 점유율 7.6% 달성 가이던스를 제시함
유럽: EV2 출시를 통한 EV 라인업 완성도 높임. 소형 해치백 차량과 신
규 HEV 차량 출시, 그리고 PBV 판매 본격화를 통한 초과 성장 목표

Compliance notice 
당 보고서 공표일 기준으로 해당 기업과 관련하여, 
 회사는 해당 종목을 1%이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 금융투자분석사와 그 배우자는 해당 기업의 주식을 보유하고 있지 않습니다.  
 당 보고서는 기관투자가 및 제 3자에게 E-mail등을 통하여 사전에 배포된 사실이 없습니다.  
 회사는 6개월간 해당 기업의 유가증권 발행과 관련 주관사로 참여하지 않았습니다. 
 당 보고서에 게재된 내용들은 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭 없이 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 분석자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위한 참고자료이며, 따라서, 본 자료에 의한 투자자의 투자결과에 대해 어떠한 목적의 증빙자료로도 사용될 수 없으며, 어떠한 경우에도 작성자 및 
당사의 허가 없이 전재, 복사 또는 대여될 수 없습니다. 무단전재 등으로 인한 분쟁발생시 법적 책임이 있음을 주지하시기 바랍니다.

[투자의견] 
종목추천 투자등급  
종목투자의견은 향후 12개월간 추천일 종가대비 해당종목의 예상 목표수익률을 의미함.  
 Buy(매수): 추천일 종가대비 +15% 이상  
 Hold(보유): 추천일 종가대비 -15% ~ 15% 내외 등락 
 Sell(매도): 추천일 종가대비 -15% 이상

 Overweight(비중확대)

주가 추이

3분기 영업이익은 지상방산 매출 감소와 일회성 비용 등의 영향으로 
기대치를 대폭 하회했습니다. 4분기 실적 회복과 중장기 실적 성장 대
비 저평가된 밸류에이션에 주목합니다. 
3Q26 Review: 영업이익 0.5조원으로 컨센서스 대폭 하회 
3분기 매출액은 6.1조원으로 전년대비 6.0% 감소했다. 항공우주 부문
과 연결자회사들 성장에도 불구하고 지상방산 매출이 전년대비 약 0.9
조원 감소한 영향이다. 지상방산 내수와 수출 매출은 각각 0.7조원, 0.6
조원으로 전년대비 28.6%, 50.4% 감소했다. 내수는 대전 사업장 생산 
중단, 수출은 4분기 납기 집중에 기인한다. 항공우주 매출은 군수사업 
물량 증가로 전년대비 9.3% 증가한 0.7조원을 기록했다. 연결 자회사
인 한화오션과 한화시스템 매출은 각각 3.4조원, 0.8조원으로 전년대비 
12.9%, 30.1% 증가했다. 영업이익은 0.5조원으로 전년대비 49.2% 감
소했으며 영업이익률은 7.4%로 6.3%P 하락했다. 한화오션과 한화시스
템의 이익 증가에도 지상방산 이익 감소와 필리조선소 적자 반영이 영
향을 미쳤다. 지상방산은 일회성 비용 약 460억원, 원화 강세, 매출 감
소에 따른 고정비 부담 증가가 복합적으로 작용했다. 세전이익은 한화
오션의 외화환산손실 영향으로 -0.2조원을 기록하며 적자전환했다.  
실적 회복과 저평가 매력에 주목할 시점 
4분기 영업이익은 1.7조원으로 추정하며 시장 기대치를 상회할 전망이
다. 지상방산 수출 인도 물량이 집중되는 영향이다. 2027년 예상 실적 
기준 P/E는 12.5배로 경쟁사 Rheinmetall(22.5배) 대비 낮다. 약 35조
원의 방산 수주잔고를 기반으로 최소 2029년까지 실적 성장이 예상되
는 만큼 저평가 매력이 높다. 신규 수주 시점과 실적에 대한 우려가 상
존하지만 현재 밸류에이션에는 상당 부분 반영된 것으로 판단한다. 미
국 중간선거 등 국방예산 관련 정책 불확실성과 환율 부담이 완화되면 
투자자들의 관심은 4분기 실적과 2027년 신규 수주로 이동할 전망이
다. 연말 주요국의 국방예산 확정은 신규 수주 시점에 대한 불확실성을 
낮추는 계기가 될 수 있다.  
투자의견 BUY, 목표주가 1,300,000원 유지 
글로벌 재무장 수요 확대에 따른 동사의 중장기 성장 전망은 여전히 
유효하다. 투자의견 BUY와 목표주가 1,300,000원을 유지한다.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 30 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (채운샘) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
한화에어로스페이스는 당사와 ‘독점규제 및 공정거래에 관한 법률’ 에 따른 계열회사 관계에 있는 법인입니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 6

한화에어로스페이스 (012450) [한화리서치]

6 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

## Document 274: Are we close to a bottom_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Are we close to a bottom_.pdf
Report source: 渣打銀行 (Market Watch)
Date: 2026-11-23
Pages: 8
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Are we close to a bottom?  
Summary 
Event: Markets corrected sharply on Friday, with the S&P 
500 down 3.7% and the Nasdaq 100 down 5.7%, while the 
VIX surged above 35. Most sectors ended lower, although 
defensives such as healthcare and consumer staples fared 
better. At the industry level, semiconductors once again led 
the losses, falling more than 10%. Gold also declined sharply 
as systematic selling added to the pressure, while bonds 
gained in flight to safety.  
Our View: While we acknowledge company-specific risks 
and downside risk to consensus AI capex estimates, much of 
this risk now appears to be priced in. We, therefore, 
recommend investors avoid overreacting and stay invested, 
while using elevated volatility through structured strategies 
and selectively building exposure to quality assets, including 
our latest opportunistic idea, US mega caps. A diversified 
multi-asset portfolio, including bonds, would serve as a 
strong foundation to weather market volatilities. 
What is driving the sell-off? 
With most valuation and sentiment metrics flashing oversold 
conditions, it appears that the market has moved beyond the 
traditional investment framework, with panic now driving the 
selloff as the typical policy support, or “Trump put,” remains 
absent so far. During previous sharp corrections, such as the 
tariff-driven sell-off in 2025, or the pandemic shock in March 
2020, a change in policy provided support to markets and 
helped trigger a sharp rebound. At this stage, not only is 
policy support missing, but headlines such as a potential SEC 
investigation into Nvidia are adding uncertainty.  
The ongoing correction remains largely centred on the AI 
theme. We have been arguing that there is 10-15% downside 
risk to consensus AI capex estimates, or roughly USD 200bn 
lower per annum. However, the roughly USD 3trn erosion in 
global semiconductor market capitalisation this month alone 
suggests the market is already discounting a more severe 
outcome. 
 US equity markets corrected sharply on Friday, 20-Nov

### Page 2

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 November 2026

What can calm investor concerns?   
We believe three factors could help: clearer visibility 
around interest rates, greater clarity on neocloud AI 
capex trends as several industry conferences take place 
over the next few weeks, and improved visibility around 
Nvidia's leadership given its critical role in the AI industry. 
Traditional support signals, such as the VIX reaching 25-30 
or market drawdowns exceeding 10%, have failed to deliver 
this time. The Nasdaq 100 is already around 20% below its 
peak, while the S&P 500 is down over 10%. While it is difficult 
to call a bottom, the S&P 500 has historically delivered strong 
positive returns on average after a 10% drawdown, over the 
next 6-12 months.  
Stay invested as structural growth is on sale 
Last month, we downgraded global equities to Neutral as 
markets transitioned into a mid-cycle environment. Even by 
our conservative standards, however, the current correction 
looks excessive. We recommend investors avoid 
overreacting and stay invested, as the fundamental backdrop 
remains supportive.  
At current levels, we estimate the market is effectively 
discounting around USD 500bn per annum of cuts to 
consensus AI capex estimates, considerably more than our 
roughly USD 200bn downside scenario. Such an outcome 
would effectively imply that most remaining AI capex is 
directed toward inference, with only limited spending on 
training. In our view, this is at odds with how the AI industry 
is progressing, particularly given the strong training pipeline 
and intensifying race toward Artificial General Intelligence. 
At the same time, AI adoption continues to expand across 
both enterprises and consumers, with US AI adoption 
reaching around 25% based on the latest October data. We 
estimate this growth could require an additional 15-20GW of 
inference capacity, equivalent to roughly USD 700-900bn per 
annum of investment. Against this backdrop, the downside 
risks to consensus capex estimates appear manageable. 
Greater clarity around these numbers at upcoming industry 
conferences should help ease investor concerns. 
While we expect sanity to eventually prevail and investors to 
stop indiscriminately selling good business models, we 
continue to advocate diversification. During the Q1 and Q3 
2026 sell-offs, we pounded the table on semiconductors and 
introduced an opportunistic idea on the industry. This time, 
while acknowledging that value has clearly emerged, we 
prefer adding exposure through more diversified 
opportunities such as US IT and our recently-introduced US 
mega-cap ideas. We still expect a moderate reduction in 
consensus AI capex estimates, which argues for remaining 
selective within semiconductors. Investors in US equities 
should also be diversified across sectors (table on right). We

investors.

## Document 300: Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-28)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Cliff Notes_ cost of living front and centre (2026-11-28).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-28
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#cliffnotes #rba #australia #inflation #us #fomc #ecb #aud #usd
Cliff Notes: cost of living front and centre
By Ryan Wells, Elliot Clarke & Illiana Jain
09:49 November 13 2026 Share
Key insights from the week that was.
In Australia, the Westpac-MI Consumer Sentiment Index fell 2.7% to 78.2 in November, its weakest
reading since late-2022 and a level historically associated with periods of severe economic stress.
Renewed cost-of-living and interest rate pressures were front and centre following another lift in

inflation, including sharply higher petrol prices, and the RBA’s November rate increase. Mortgage
holders are feeling the squeeze most acutely, with sentiment across this group falling to a record low.

### Page 2

Consumers have become noticeably more downbeat on the near-term economic outlook – the relevant
sub-index dropping 10.2% to be 22% below its long-run average. Job-loss fears also continue to edge
up.
Businesses are similarly troubled. The latest NAB business survey showed confidence slipped further
to –15 in October as conditions held near their weakest level since the pandemic. Of concern for the
outlook, forward orders fell to –12, also the weakest read since the pandemic, and capital expenditure

intentions deteriorated. Purchase costs continue to grow materially faster than selling prices, further
compressing already tight margins.
Housing provides a clear example of how caution and tighter financial conditions can jolt activity. The
value of new dwelling finance approvals plunged 15% in Q3, the largest quarterly decline in the series’
history, taking approvals 25% lower year-to-date. Investor lending bore the brunt of the adjustment,
falling 30% over the quarter as higher rates combined with the housing tax changes announced in May.

Owner-occupier lending also weakened, however, with first-home buyers proving particularly sensitive
to the deterioration in affordability and market conditions.
Taken together, the data points to a further loss of momentum across the domestic economy,
consistent with Westpac’s expectation for subdued growth and concern over downside activity risks.
Meanwhile, in the US, October’s headline CPI met our expectations but at 0.4% was a touch above the

consensus view. Energy and food both contributed meaningfully to the result, but core inflation was
also marginally stronger than forecast, +0.3%. Core goods inflation has accelerated over the past four
months to average a gain of 0.2% as businesses look to pass tariffs, tech and energy input cost
inflation through. Shelter meanwhile continued to rise at an above-average pace, gaining 0.3% in
October.
The market impact of the October CPI report was accentuated by hawkish remarks from Fed Governor

Waller prior to the release. He expressed confidence in economic activity and the labour market while
showing significant concern over the strength and persistence of inflation, focusing on the passthrough
of energy and technology input inflation. His conclusion for policy was clear: "if, as I expect, the
monthly rate of inflation improves in October, I am leaning toward supporting an increase in our policy
rate of 25 basis points at the FOMC's December meeting"; but "if the October CPI inflation data out

later today are similar to the past two monthly readings—with core CPI inflation around 0.3 percent or
even higher—then a stronger policy move may be needed".
Regional Fed Presidents Goolsbee, Logan and Hammack also kept the focus on inflation risks and
stated or implied the FOMC needs to act. Market participants have responded to this week’s updates,
pricing a 25bp hike in for the December and January meetings, and a cumulative 3.5 hikes by
September. We believe this pricing overstates the need for policy tightening given core inflation ex

shelter remains relatively benign at 2.4% on a six-month annualised basis and the labour market is
balanced; but it will take time for price concerns to subside.
While we agree that the US economy is strong in aggregate, US households are under pressure from
the cost of living, and their investment decision making is constrained by borrowing costs. Highlighting

### Page 3

Related articles
this, US existing home sales fell 5.5% in October after a downwardly revised 1.0% gain in September,
leaving the level of sales below the cycle lows of 2009 and 2010. Our November Market Outlook will
provide a full update on our expectations for the US and the world when released on Westpac IQ this
afternoon.

## Document 308: COP31 Takeaways_ Collapse or reset_ (2026-11-25)

Source PDF: all report pdf/COP31 Takeaways_ Collapse or reset_ (2026-11-25).pdf
Report source: DB Research
Date: 2026-11-25
Pages: 21
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

COP31 Takeaways: Collapse or reset?
Climate diplomacy has stalled. COP31 in Antalya closed without a headline agreement, something that has not happened
since 2009. Talks on moving away from fossil fuels proved too divisive, and progress on funding for developing countries
to adapt was limited. Yet we see a necessary reset rather than a collapse. Electrification, a key step in the energy
transition, has support, and coalitions of willing countries may press ahead without waiting for the consensus on climate

Deutsche Bank
Research
Debbie Jones | Andrew Dixon
S U S T A I N A B I L I T Y  |  T H E M A T I C  R E S E A R C H  |  N O V E M B E R  2 0 2 6
10 key takeaways from COP31 (see pages 2–5 for a summary)

### Page 2

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
COP 3 1  TAK EAW AYS   |   I NTR OD U CTI ON AND  OV ER V I EW
A collapse or reset? Why did COP31 talks break down and what happens next
2
The COP process stalled this year, but a reset may be on the horizon
The UN climate summit in Antalya (Türkiye) ended on Sunday without a headline
agreement, after a draft was rejected as too weak on fossil fuels. Many are calling
the outcome a collapse of the COP process, which brings together almost 200

nations to agree shared climate action. At the same time, the science now suggests
the world will overshoot the 1.5°C global warming limit set in the Paris Agreement,
and the pressure for tougher action is growing. The outcome is a headwind to
climate diplomacy, but it also suggests to us that elements of the COP process need
to evolve to ensure compatibility with the current economic and political backdrop.
A growing bloc is no longer willing to accept weak / no language on fossil fuels

The headline agreement or “final text” is made by consensus. While this is open to a
degree of interpretation, what is clear is that a few objections can block a deal. The
EU, Norway and several vulnerable countries did just that, arguing the draft fell short
of what is needed to tackle climate change. It was the first time since 2009 that a
COP has ended without an overarching agreement. More than 130 countries backed
stronger wording on fossil fuels, up from about 80 at COP30 last year. Resistance to

the language also grew, with some oil-producing countries pushing instead for a
bigger role for carbon capture technology, which widened the divide.
Poorer countries were left disappointed on climate finance commitments
Developing countries had hoped for clarity on last year’s agreement to triple funding
for adaptation finance. Strained public finances in developed countries made
progress more challenging. The US withdrawal from the Paris Agreement, together

with cuts to its overseas climate funding, has left a gap others are struggling to fill.
Private capital will be needed to fill the gap, but the economics must work
Public money alone will not deliver the $300bn a year that developed countries
agreed at COP29 to mobilise by 2035. Having argued the draft was too weak to
address climate change, Europe will be expected to help mobilise private capital at
scale. That means policy support and steps to reduce investors' risk, so that returns

are attractive enough to draw capital in greater numbers.
Voluntary action will also need to set a new standard for progress
We expect groups of like-minded countries and voluntary plans, which will be
intertwined with COP discussions without being dependent on the formal consensus
process, to increasingly drive progress on divisive topics. Examples include several
country specific Santa Marta coalition national plans to move away from fossil fuels

and Brazil's voluntary transition roadmap. In this respect, failure to reach consensus
at COP31 may prompt the change of pace needed for countries to move forward.
Electrification offers common ground
Türkiye's voluntary “35-by-35” pledge, which aims to lift electricity's share of global
final energy use from c.23% to 35% by 2035, was the clearest common ground in
Antalya. More than 80 countries endorsed it, including China and the EU, and it drew

strong business backing. Critics note it does not explicitly require clean power, but it
encourages it, and that flexibility helped widen support while fitting energy-security
goals. We expect focus on electrification at future COPs, with attention shifting from
endorsements to delivery, such as investment in grids and the electrification of
transport and industry, and to whether more countries can be brought on board.
What happens next?
Bonn’s mid-year talks will test whether negotiators can rebuild consensus on the

divisive issues. COP32 in Addis Ababa in 2027 will then turn to delivery, with a focus
on adaptation finance (a focus for many African nations). COP33 in 2028, which
concludes the second global stocktake and thus shapes countries’ next climate plans
due in 2030, still has no host: China reportedly declined to bid and India withdrew its
offer. A swift decision matters beyond logistics. COP31 may have faltered partly
because Türkiye and Australia had less than a year to prepare, which didn’t provide

enough time to build the necessary consensus.
This report is constructed in two parts. The first (pages 2-5) sets out our top 10 takeaways
from COP31 in short form, for readers who want our high-level thoughts and observations.
The second covers each takeaway in full, followed by a look at COP next steps, and an
appendix explaining key COP terms mentioned in this note.

### Page 3

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
COP 3 1  TAK EAW AYS  |  TOP  1 0  (1  o f 3 )
Antalya - 10 takeaways from COP31
3
01
02
03
NO HEADLINE AGREEMENT
Consensus agreement tested; the COP
process will be forced to evolve
See page 7
– Amid questions over the effectiveness of multilateralism, COP31 delegates failed to agree on a headline agreement (i.e.,
the final cover text). This is important as the text is typically seen as a key signal for the global climate agenda. While this

outcome is a setback for the COP goal of collective climate progress, the result will force a rethink of the process.
– This result is disappointing for climate advocates and developing nations, but the outcome is also acknowledgement that
the current process is not delivering at the pace required to address the now anticipated temperature overshoot. We still
see a role for consensus decision making, but it now won’t be relied upon. Instead, we expect to see greater focus on

voluntary action and “coalitions of the willing”, whereby like-minded countries seek action on divisive issues.
MORE SUPPORT FOR FOSSIL FUEL
TRANSITION MET WITH RESISTANCE
Coalitions of the willing are emerging
and building transition roadmaps
See page 8
– Despite the lack of agreement on the final cover text, we took away from COP that there is a notable faction of support
for a transition away from fossil fuels. This is something to watch. Reports suggest that more than 130 countries backed

or would not object to stronger fossil fuel language, up from more than 80 at COP30. Energy security worries and
heightened concern over rising temperatures likely played a role in this shift.
– Away from the formal negotiations, Brazil’s COP30 presidency presented its voluntary global roadmap for transitioning
away from fossil fuels. While not binding, notable countries including India and Nigeria are reportedly welcoming it.
France, Germany, Finland, Denmark and the Netherlands have also published national transition roadmaps. More could

follow ahead of the second voluntary Santa Marta coalition meeting next July, co-hosted by Tuvalu and Ireland. This will
be an early test of whether high-level support translates into action, policy and investment.
STRAINED CLIMATE FINANCE
Developing countries are frustrated
with the COP process, while developed
countries are constrained
See pages 9–10
– Climate finance, including adaptation, was the priority for most developing countries at COP31, but progress was limited.

A key ask was clearer language defining the pledge to triple adaptation finance by 2035, agreed last year in Belém
without a headline figure or baseline year. An early call to solidify this as a $120bn annual target was dropped from later
drafts, though this was always the most likely outcome given the EU had ruled out new pledges.
– The shortfall in climate finance delivery against existing pledges is most concerning to the Global South, where climate

impacts are most severe and the capacity to fund adaptation is weakest. Developing countries often can’t meet their
Nationally Determined Contribution (NDC) goals to lower emissions without better access to finance, and this extends
beyond the amount available, with accessibility and conditions (particularly around debt burdens) a barrier.
– There was a breakthrough on technical aspects around refining a set of 59 indicators to measure adaptation progress

under the Global Goal on Adaptation. Once fully detailed, these indicators will provide important signals as to the areas
where finance for adaptation projects is most urgently required.

### Page 4

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
Antalya - 10 takeaways from COP31
4
06
COP 3 1  TAK EAW AYS  |  TOP  1 0  (2  o f 3 )
07
05
IPCC TIMELINE IN QUESTION
No agreed timeline for the reports key
to the 2028 global stocktake
See page 12
– A recurring concern among attendees was a potential delay to the IPCC’s next assessment reports, for two reasons. First,
the reports are key to informing the second global stocktake, concluding in 2028. Second, our discussions suggest that

climate deterioration is happening faster than initially expected, thus delay risks decisions resting on out-of-date science.
– Even if there is agreement to produce the IPCC report on time, it may not be fully accurate, as the timeline may limit
contributions from scientists in developing countries, such as Kenya, who are struggling to secure the funding and
capacity needed to contribute within the shorter timeframe.
TRADE TENSIONS CONTINUE
Pushback against the EU’s CBAM and

other unilateral trade measures
See page 13
– Developing nations, including China, India and the African Group, pushed back against unilateral climate-related trade
measures, with the EU’s Carbon Border Adjustment Mechanism (CBAM) a particular target. We expect trade to remain a
source of friction in climate diplomacy, while cleaner producers should gain a global trade advantage.
ELECTRIFICATION
The clearest common ground, with 80+
countries backing 35-by-35

See page 14
– Electrification was the clearest area of common ground in Antalya. The voluntary “35-by-35” pledge aims to raise
electricity’s share of global final energy use from about 23% today to 35% by 2035, and was endorsed by more than 80
countries, including China and the EU, with strong business support. Current and stated policies point to only 28–29% by
2035, so a gap remains.
– While aligned with broader energy security goals, the electrification pledge drew criticism for not requiring clean energy.

We viewed the ambiguity as a way to facilitate broader agreement and grow the infrastructure needed for a more
meaningful shift away from fossil fuels. We expect continued building on this goal at future COP events.
PRESSURE ON EUROPE
Having rejected the cover text, Europe
will be expected to lead action
See page 11
04
– Exiting COP31, we expect more diplomatic pressure on the European nations to back up their stance that the COP31
draft text did not go far enough to address climate change. The EU, UK, Norway and Switzerland all either formally

rejected or voiced opposition to the draft text. Countries choosing to walk away will be expected to show how they can
deliver the ambition they say the text lacked.
– Public finance constraints (a point of contention and a limiting factor for ambition at this year’s COP) will mean taking
steps to enable private capital, as well as driving the regulation needed to mobilise it. At issue is that investors face
limited transparency, hard-to-assess risk and a high cost of capital, which guarantees and other risk-sharing tools could

help reduce. As one participant noted, private finance is being asked to show up at COP. They need a reason to do so.

### Page 5

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
5
Antalya - 10 takeaways from COP31
09
10
08
COP 3 1  TAK EAW AYS  |  TOP  1 0  (3  o f 3 )
AI & CLIMATE
A missed opportunity
See page 15
– AI was a missed opportunity. While the power demands of data centres were acknowledged as a driver of increased
energy demand, discussion of AI’s role in advancing climate goals was surprisingly limited: just 2% of the more than 4,000
Blue Zone events we analysed had a notable focus on AI. Given the strong presence of climate technology at COP, we had

expected AI to feature far more prominently.
– The Antalya AI Pledge drew a muted response with only seven governments among c.80 backers. Major US AI firms
stayed away, though Alibaba signed up. Launched by the Presidency with the ITU, it serves as a voluntary framework
committing signatories to measurable action to use AI for climate goals while curbing its own energy and resource
footprint. As the focus on data centres increases, we expect this topic to feature more prominently at COP.

US WITHDRAWAL HAD AN IMPACT, BUT
DEMOCRATS SHOW UP
The US exit has left a clear dent, but
Democratic governors express
optimism
See pages 16-17
– The Trump administration sent no official delegation for a second year. After exiting the Paris Agreement, the US is set to
become the first country to leave the UNFCCC, the treaty that underpins global climate diplomacy. More than 120 US
state, local and business leaders attended COP, but as observers without a seat at the negotiating table. This absence

was felt across negotiating rooms, with observers highlighting the key role previously played by the US in bridging
divides. For the next few years at least, this dynamic is set to continue.
– The Democrats showed up with a message of optimism. The America Is All In coalition held several panels at COP,
highlighting that following the midterms, Democratic governors will lead states home to roughly three-quarters of the US
population, supporting the view that state-level policy will remain a key driver of US clean energy and industrial

investment. While we tend to agree, the magnitude of the benefit isn’t entirely clear.
NATURE, CARBON MARKETS & OCEANS
Future progress needed, but some
action was notable
See page 18
– Warming oceans and rising sea levels are a growing economic risk, damaging coastal infrastructure and fisheries and
raising insurance costs. COP31 delivered momentum but no negotiated ocean outcome. Funding was modest, including a
CAD$14mn commitment from Canada to the Ocean Risk and Resilience Action Alliance.

– Forest finance received a boost, with Canada pledging up to $392.5mn to the Tropical Forest Forever Facility (TFFF),
launched at COP30, taking total sponsor pledges to $8.3bn. The TFFF matters because it pays countries to keep tropical
forests standing, making protection more valuable than clearing. This voluntary effort was launched by Brazil at COP30.
– Carbon markets inched forward as attention turned to putting Article 6, the Paris Agreement’s rulebook for trading

emissions cuts between countries, into practice. The first version of the international Article 6.2 registry went live, and
Norway agreed to buy up to 12 MtCO₂e of credits from Indonesia. On 12 November, 14 governments signed the Antalya
Statement on Mobilising Private Finance for Climate Action, with a playbook of eight policy levers to grow corporate
demand for high-integrity credits. Corporates and investors still stress the need for robust credits that deliver the

promised emissions reductions and limit greenwashing risk.

### Page 7

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
TAK EAW AY 1   |   MU L TI L ATER AL I S M
No cover text: COP31 delegates fail to agree, testing multilateralism and the consensus model
7
Amid questions over the effectiveness of multilateralism, COP31 delegates failed to agree the final cover text, typically seen as a key signal for the global climate agenda. While this no
doubt sets back timelines for near-term implementation of agreed climate goals, it will likely force the COP process to evolve. This result is disappointing for climate advocates, but the

outcome is also acknowledgement that the current process is not delivering action at the pace required to address the temperature overshoot. We don’t think multilateralism as part
of the COP process is dead, but it now won’t be relied upon. Instead, we expect to see greater focus on “coalitions of the willing”, whereby like-minded countries seek progress on
divisive issues.
Formal objections by several parties to the cover text proposed at COP31 meant

consensus could not be achieved
Decision making at COP requires consensus among parties. What this means in practice
is open to interpretation, particularly whether objection from a single country can block
an agreement. Control of proceedings rests with the COP President. At COP31, however,
formal objections from several parties, including the EU, Vanuatu, Panama and Norway,
meant the consensus test was clearly not satisfied. With the Presidency confirming no

further text would follow, COP31 ended on 22 November without a cover agreement –
the first time since COP15 in Copenhagen in 2009.
Multilateralism has produced landmark results, but the backdrop has changed
The Paris Agreement (COP21 in 2015, which set the goal of limiting warming to well
below 2°C while pursuing 1.5°C) and the UAE Consensus (COP28 in 2023, agreement to
“transition away from fossil fuels”) were remarkable achievements, agreed by almost 200

nations. Since then, the world has changed. With delivery requiring financial backing,
climate change has become more politically fractious, as geopolitical tensions, trade
disputes, energy-security concerns and cost-of-living pressures have pushed
governments to prioritise domestic competitiveness over collective action. The US
withdrawal from the Paris Agreement has further weakened the multilateral push.
Pre-COP diplomacy was challenged given the short time frame

Some contrast Antalya with Dubai, where the COP28 presidency spent much of the year
building consensus before the summit. Some felt Australia was seen as the junior
partner (despite leading the formal negotiations), which created confusion. The Türkiye–
Australia split was only agreed at COP30 in November 2025, leaving under a year to
prepare. The lateness and dual structure may also have blurred accountability during the
process.
Looking ahead to next year, the mid-year talks in Bonn and COP32 in Addis Ababa

will seek to overcome the divides which proved too large to bridge at COP31
With the African Group frustrated that adaptation finance was overshadowed by the
fossil fuel debate, we expect a more concrete definition of the pledge to triple adaptation
finance to be a clear priority for the Ethiopian presidency. Meanwhile, the COP33 host for
2028 remains unclear, after China reportedly decided not to bid. This matters, as COP33
will conclude the Paris Agreement’s second Global Stocktake.

Multilateralism as part of the COP process will continue, but we expect greater
emphasis on “coalitions of the willing”
Voluntary structures are arguably more suitable for achieving certain goals when
resistance to climate objectives is strong. The new Santa Marta coalition is a clear
example, with 50+ countries advancing fossil fuel transition planning after COP30 in
Belém failed to include such language. Because it sits outside the formal consensus

process, members can move forward on language that could not be agreed in Antalya.
While the cover text failure will dominate the media headlines, we would highlight
some notable announcements outside the formal negotiations
– Non-CO₂ GHGs: $480mn+ of new philanthropic funding at the leaders’ methane
summit; 35 countries signed Germany’s pledge to cut power-sector emissions of
sulphur hexafluoride (SF6).
– Clean energy: Türkiye announced a $15bn clean energy deal with China. Amazon

announced $7bn of additional investment in Indian renewables by 2030 and a 65%
cut in the carbon intensity of its electricity by 2030 (vs 2019).
– Industry: Antalya Green Industrialisation Compact (aims to drive investment in cleaner
steel and cement) and UNIDO’s Global Green Industrialization Agenda both launched.

### Page 8

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
TAK EAW AY 2   |   F OS S I L  F U EL S
The fossil fuel dispute blocked agreement, but support for a transition is growing
8
The fossil fuel language dispute proved too big to overcome, but there is notable support for a transition away from fossil fuels. This is something to watch. Reports suggest that more
than 130 countries backed stronger fossil fuel language, up from more than 80 at COP30. In addition, Brazil’s COP30 presidency presented its voluntary roadmap for transitioning away

from fossil fuels at COP31, outside the negotiations. It invited countries to set national plans by 2028, with the main cuts to coal, oil and gas expected after 2031. While not binding,
countries including India and Nigeria are reportedly welcoming it. Furthermore, a handful of European countries, including France, Germany, Finland, Denmark and the Netherlands,
have published national roadmaps, while Brazil is preparing its own. More could follow ahead of the second Santa Marta meeting next July, co-hosted by Tuvalu and Ireland.

The UAE Consensus pledge to “transition away from fossil fuels” was a historic
commitment, yet building on it has been a point of contention ever since
The UAE Consensus was agreed at COP28 (2023), and represented the first call to
“transition away from fossil fuels” in a COP final text. However, attempts to take this
pledge further or even simply repeat it have been the source of major disagreement at
every COP since. Talks last year in Belém came close to collapse over this issue, and this

time in Antalya proved to be a tipping point for nations in favour of stronger climate
action.
With a significant increase in backing for stronger fossil fuel transition language at
COP31, supporting nations chose to make a stand
Reports suggest that more than 130 countries backed (or would not object to) stronger
fossil fuel language, up from more than 80 at COP30, with this significant numerical shift
in favour of transition language likely a result of increased concern over fossil fuel

dependencies, scientific confirmation of a temperature overshoot, and increased
organisation among nations in favour of such an outcome.
A group of fossil fuel producing nations maintained their red line that a fossil fuel
mention was not acceptable
While an earlier draft of the cover decision did include a reference to action to phase out
fossil fuels, this was removed. Nations including Saudi Arabia and Russia were opposed
to any such mention, with the latter claiming that cutting fossil fuel production faster

than demand could result in “fuel poverty”. Nations led by the EU, however, refused to
alter their positions, with German Environment Minister Schneider stating “a step
backwards compared with previous climate-protection decisions is the last thing
Germany can afford”. In the end, neither faction was willing to give ground.
A dispute over the inclusion in the cover decision of language on carbon capture,
utilisation and storage technologies ran parallel to the broader fossil fuel debate

These technologies (known as CCUS) capture CO2 before it reaches the atmosphere and
store it underground or use it in other processes. At COP31, the Arab Group (led by
Saudi Arabia) pushed strongly for their inclusion in the cover decision, with one
representative stating “it makes no sense that concrete solutions will be taken off the
table”. Other nations, however, pushed back, with the EU claiming that the inclusion of
CCUS would send a “signal contrary to the need of cutting emissions”. Some

stakeholders opposed it on the grounds that the technology has yet to be proven as
effective / viable on a larger scale.
The IPCC does recognise the role of carbon capture in lowering net emissions
With or without a place in the COP process, the technology is expanding. It is increasingly
seen as a pathway for oil-producing nations that also have the capital to invest in it. In
our view, this is likely another area where different factions will break off and pursue

their own goals away from the formal COP process.
Geoengineering was another technological solution discussed
Geoengineering aims to limit the impact of rising temperatures rather than directly
reduce emissions. One example is stratospheric aerosol injection, which would release
reflective particles into the upper atmosphere to mimic the cooling effect of large
volcanic eruptions. However, the technique remains largely untested and the
conversation is not mainstream at COP, but could gain more attention as adaptation

costs swell.  In Singapore, water agency PUB and US start-up Equatic have built Equatic-1
in Tuas, set to be the world’s largest facility for removing CO2 from the ocean.

### Page 9

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
TAK EAW AY 3   |   CL I MATE & AD AP TATI ON F I NANCE
Climate finance delivery fell short, with adaptation finance overshadowed
9
Climate finance, including adaptation, was the priority for most developing countries at COP31, but progress was limited. The shortfall is felt most in the Global South, where impacts
are severe and capacity is weakest. The key ask was clearer language on the Belém pledge to triple adaptation finance by 2035, lacking a figure or baseline. An early draft’s $120bn

target was dropped, and the EU ruled out new pledges, preferring delivery of existing ones. The main step was the Adaptation Fund’s switch to serving only the Paris Agreement,
though its value hinges on Article 6.4 markets scaling. With public finance constrained, private capital is needed, with Europe expected to support incentives to mobilise it.
Adaptation finance was a priority for the Global South, but progress fell short of
their demands
Sentiment from the Chair of the African Group of Negotiators after talks collapsed

reflected this. They claimed that the fossil fuel dispute was “over-hyped”, adding that this
meant their demands for greater focus on adaptation finance were “overshadowed”.
While an initial draft text contained reference to a $120bn annual adaptation target for
2035 (which would have reflected a tripling of the previous $40bn annual goal for 2025),
this did not appear in subsequent versions (some sources suggested that France instead
pushed for a $60bn figure, which would represent a tripling vs. a 2019 baseline). The

significance of an adaptation-specific goal (as part of the broader $1.3tn climate finance
ambition) lies in the fact that adaptation projects often lack a clear source of returns,
thus are much more dependent on public financing relative to mitigation projects.
Defining the adaptation tripling pledge was expected to be challenging
Prior to the conference, the EU ruled out making any new finance pledges, and this
sentiment was repeated by high-level EU officials at the event. They argued that the

focus should be on delivering against existing pledges, while pointing out the significant
climate finance contributions already made by the EU. With clarity on the adaptation
commitment a key priority for the African Group of Negotiators, this will likely be a focus
next year at COP32 in Addis Ababa.
There was agreement to move forward refining the adaptation indicators, which
will help identify where finance is most needed
Last year at COP30, countries agreed on a set of 59 adaptation indicators to measure

progress under the Global Goal on Adaptation. At COP31, this process moved forward
with an agreement over the composition of technical committees to refine these
indicators. Completing these indicators will provide standardized metrics to support
identification of adaptation finance needs.
A technical breakthrough on the Adaptation Fund at COP31 could unlock more
adaptation finance via Article 6.4 carbon markets, if they can scale
Transitioning the Adaptation Fund (which provides developing countries with finance for

adaptation projects) to exclusively serve the Paris Agreement (having previously served
both the Paris Agreement and the now largely superseded Kyoto Protocol), was a
positive outcome at COP31. The significance of this development lies in the fact that it
enables the fund to receive a mandatory 5% share of proceeds from carbon credit
trading conducted via a centralised UNFCCC mechanism under Article 6.4 of the Paris
Agreement. At present, however, this market remains small, meaning the long-term

significance of this breakthrough will depend on the scaling of trading under Article 6.4.
Continued public financing constraints will mean a greater role for the private
sector; yet there are clear barriers to overcome.
Current geopolitical and economic realities mean that public finance from developed
countries will remain constrained. Private capital will therefore need to be leaned upon if
the world is to limit (and reverse) the projected overshoot of 1.5°C, which will require

government support, transparency and de-risking to ensure finance is accessible to
developing nations. Discussions at COP31 highlighted examples where this is working in
bankable areas such as grids and industrial decarbonisation with long-term offtake,
backed by World Bank Group guarantees and first-loss capital that helped mobilise
c.$112bn of private finance last fiscal year (covers broader mobilization not just for
climate projects). However, only c.$3bn reached low-income countries; reflecting

sentiment that much of climate finance for developing countries flows to middle-income
nations. Adaptation is less mature, lacking standardised ways to monetise avoided
losses, and project transparency remains an issue. These challenges are compounded by
gaps between investor requirements and developing country governments’ ability to
meet them, particularly in capacity constrained nations.

### Page 10

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
TAK EAW AY 3   |   CL I MATE & AD AP TATI ON F I NANCE
COP31 delivered little on climate finance, so COP32 becomes the delivery test
COP31 left the climate finance question largely unanswered. Talks on who should pay stalled and a proposed $120bn target for adaptation funding was dropped. New public pledges fell by
more than 70% in 2026 to about $11.7bn a year, with the EU ruling out fresh pledges. We expect reaching the $300bn and $1.3tn-a-year goals for 2035 will depend on private capital and

development bank reform. This slide sets out where each goal stands and the hurdles for COP32.

Why it matters: The Fund is the only dedicated channel for
irreversible climate impacts, yet it has no annual funding target and
resources remain far below demand.

against a $342m first-round allocation (plus $21.6m set aside for a
Nepal flood pilot). COP31 only noted the shortfall and urged pledges

### Page 11

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
TAK EAW AY 4   |   P R ES S U R E ON EU R OP E
Europe will be expected to show how ambition can be delivered given calls for greater ambition
11
GDP, Emissions and UNFCCC Classification by Country
(Wealthier Non-Annex I countries are labelled, as is the USA)
Source: World Bank, EDGAR, Deutsche Bank Research
Exiting COP31, there will be more pressure on the European nations to back up their stance that the COP31 final draft text (i.e., headline agreement) did not go far enough to address

climate change. The EU, UK, Norway and Switzerland all either formally rejected or voiced opposition to the draft text. Countries choosing to walk away will be expected to show how
they could deliver the ambition they say the text lacked, including finance for developing countries. With public finance constrained (a point of contention and driver of constraint at
this year’s COP), this means enabling private capital and driving the regulation needed to mobilise it.

The collapse of COP31 cover text negotiations should not be viewed as a shock in
isolation; arguably, this outcome has been building up over recent COPs.
Last year at COP30, EU negotiators reportedly considered walking away from talks over
dissatisfaction regarding the lack of fossil fuel language, yet they ultimately decided to accept
the deal after receiving limited concessions, with a broader sense among EU figures at the
time that it was better to keep the process moving forwards as opposed to collapsing it

entirely. However, post-COP30 discussions among EU members reportedly included a push
from some for clearer guidance on when to walk away, meaning COP31’s outcome was seen
as possible.
Having rejected the final cover text on the grounds that it was not ambitious enough,
Europe will face pressure to show how to deliver these stronger outcomes
By taking a hard line on including fossil fuel transition language in the text, Europe will be
expected to support the development of incentives and frameworks that enable more

ambitious outcomes, particularly with respect to incentivising private capital. The role of
multilateral development banks will likely be a focus, while guarantees and risk-sharing are
viewed as some of the most effective methods of scaling up private finance involvement.
Reports that China would bid to host COP33 proved false
China remains the world’s largest emitter, accounting for around a third of global emissions,
yet it has made considerable progress, often highlighted in detail at its COP pavilion. Its CO2

emissions have been broadly flat or falling since early 2024, driven by record growth in wind
and solar. China clearly wants the net zero transition to move forward, given its significant
investment in climate technology, on which the rest of the world, including Europe and the
Global South, is increasingly reliant. Notably, however, it decided not to bid to host COP33 in
2028, despite hopes from some that such an event would help fill the leadership gap left by

the US.

Debbie Jones  |  COP31 Takeaways  |  November 2026
TAK EAW AY 5   |   S CI ENCE & THE I P CC
No agreement on the timeline for the IPCC reports key to informing the 2028 stocktake

1.5°C
